<div class="col-sm order-1">
      <h2 class="chapter_title">CHAPTER 123A. SCHOOL DISTRICTS; FORMS FOR ORGANIZING</h2>
      
      <div class="sr" id="stat.123A.001"><b><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.001">123A.001</a></b> MS 2006 [Renumbered 15.001]</div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.01">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.01 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.01.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this chapter, the words defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.05">120A.05</a>, have the same meanings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.01.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Teacher.</span></h2>
          <p>For purposes of this chapter, "teacher" means a teacher as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.1">122A.40, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.01.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.3.1.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 3 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.2.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 2</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">STATE-APPROVED ALTERNATIVE PROGRAMS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.05">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.05 STATE-APPROVED ALTERNATIVE PROGRAM ORGANIZATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.05.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Governance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A district may establish an area learning center, alternative learning program, or contract alternative in accordance with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.68#stat.124D.68.3">124D.68, subdivision 3</a>, paragraph (d), and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.69">124D.69</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) An area learning center is encouraged to cooperate with a service cooperative, an intermediate school district, a local education and employment transitions partnership, public and private secondary and postsecondary institutions, public agencies, businesses, and foundations. Except for a district located in a city of the first class, an area learning center must be established in cooperation with other districts and must serve the geographic area of at least two districts. An area learning center must provide comprehensive educational services to enrolled secondary students throughout the year, including a daytime school within a school or separate site for both high school and middle school level students.</p>
          <p>(c) An alternative learning program may serve the students of one or more districts, may designate which grades are served, and may make program hours and a calendar optional.</p>
          <p>(d) A contract alternative is an alternative learning program operated by a private organization that has contracted with a school district to provide educational services for students under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.68#stat.124D.68.2">124D.68, subdivision 2</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.05.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reserve revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Each district that is a member of an area learning center or alternative learning program must reserve revenue in an amount equal to the sum of (1) at least 90 and no more than 100 percent of the district average general education revenue per adjusted pupil unit minus an amount equal to the product of the formula allowance according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.2">126C.10, subdivision 2</a>, times .0466, calculated without basic skills revenue, local optional revenue, and transportation sparsity revenue, times the number of pupil units attending an area learning center or alternative learning program under this section, plus (2) the amount of basic skills revenue generated by pupils attending the area learning center or alternative learning program. The amount of reserved revenue under this subdivision may only be spent on program costs associated with the area learning center or alternative learning program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.05.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Access to services.</span></h2>
          <p>A state-approved alternative program shall have access to the district's regular education programs, special education programs, technology facilities, and staff. It may contract with individuals or postsecondary institutions. It shall seek the involvement of community education programs, postsecondary institutions, interagency collaboratives, culturally based organizations, mutual assistance associations, and other community resources, businesses, and other federal, state, and local public agencies.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.05.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Nonresident pupils.</span></h2>
          <p>A pupil who does not reside in the district may attend a state-approved alternative program without consent of the school board of the district of residence.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.05.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.34.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.20.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 20</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.138.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.4.12.0">1996 c 412 art 4 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.2.32.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 2 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.100.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 100</a>,101,104; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.33.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.2.4.0">1999 c 241 art 2 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.1.2.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.29.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.15.1.0">2014 c 312 art 15 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.06">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.06 STATE-APPROVED ALTERNATIVE PROGRAMS AND SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.06.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Program focus.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The programs and services of a state-approved alternative program must focus on academic and learning skills, applied learning opportunities, trade and vocational skills, work-based learning opportunities, work experience, youth service to the community, transition services, and English language and literacy programs for children whose primary language is a language other than English. Applied learning, work-based learning, and service learning may best be developed in collaboration with a local education and transitions partnership, culturally based organizations, mutual assistance associations, or other community resources. In addition to offering programs, the state-approved alternative program shall coordinate the use of other available educational services, special education services, social services, health services, and postsecondary institutions in the community and services area.</p>
          <p>(b) Consistent with the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.40">121A.40</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.56">121A.56</a>, a school district may provide an alternative education program for a student who is within the compulsory attendance age under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.20">120A.20</a>, and who is involved in severe or repeated disciplinary action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.06.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">People to be served.</span></h2>
          <p>A state-approved alternative program shall provide programs for secondary pupils. A center may also provide programs and services for elementary and secondary pupils who are not attending the state-approved alternative program to assist them in being successful in school. A center shall use research-based best practices for serving English learners and their parents, taking into account the variations in students' backgrounds and needs and the amount of time and the staff resources necessary for students to overcome gaps in their education and to develop English proficiency and work-related skills. An individualized education program team may identify a state-approved alternative program as an appropriate placement to the extent a state-approved alternative program can provide the student with the appropriate special education services described in the student's plan. Pupils eligible to be served are those who qualify under the graduation incentives program in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.68#stat.124D.68.2">124D.68, subdivision 2</a>, those enrolled under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.02">124D.02</a>, subdivision 2, or those pupils who are eligible to receive special education services under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.03">125A.03</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.24">125A.24</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.65">125A.65</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.06.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Hours of instruction exemption.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, the area learning center programs must be available throughout the entire year.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.06.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Granting a diploma.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon successful completion of the area learning center program, a pupil is entitled to receive a high school diploma. The pupil may elect to receive a diploma from either the district of residence or the district in which the area learning center is located or the intermediate district or educational cooperative responsible for the area learning center program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.06.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.35.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 35</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.6.15.0">1988 c 718 art 6 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/146/#laws.5.16.0">1993 c 146 art 5 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.21.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.3.17.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 3 s 17</a>; art 6 s 14; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 104</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.34.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 34</a>,35; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.2.6.0">1999 c 241 art 2 s 6</a>,7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.1.1.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 1 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/263/#laws.1.2.0">2006 c 263 art 1 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.30.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 30</a>; 1Sp<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2011/0/11/#laws.3.12.0">2011 c 11 art 3 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/239/#laws.1.33.0">2012 c 239 art 1 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.1.28.0">2014 c 272 art 1 s 28</a>; art 3 s 22; art 6 s 1</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.07">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.07 RESOURCE CENTER FOR OTHER PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <p>An area learning center must serve as a resource for other districts, educational, community, and business organizations. The center may charge a fee for these services. The following services shall be provided for a region or the state:</p>
        <p>(1) information and research for alternative programs;</p>
        <p>(2) regional or state workshops on awareness, identification, programs, and support for these pupils;</p>
        <p>(3) recommendations for staff qualifications to ensure the most qualified staff can be selected for the programs; and</p>
        <p>(4) recommendations for successful learning programs for special education students placed in an alternative setting.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.07.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.36.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 36</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 104</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.36.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 36</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.08">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.08 STATE-APPROVED ALTERNATIVE PROGRAM FUNDING.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.08.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Outside sources for resources and services.</span></h2>
          <p>A state-approved alternative program may accept:</p>
          <p>(1) resources and services from postsecondary institutions serving state-approved alternative program pupils;</p>
          <p>(2) resources from Workforce Investment Act of 1998, Public Law 105-220 programs, including funding for jobs skills training for various groups and the percentage reserved for education;</p>
          <p>(3) resources from the Department of Human Services and county welfare funding;</p>
          <p>(4) resources from a local education and employment transitions partnership; or</p>
          <p>(5) private resources, foundation grants, gifts, corporate contributions, and other grants.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.08.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">General education aid.</span></h2>
          <p>Payment of general education aid for nonresident pupils enrolled in area learning centers and alternative learning programs must be made according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.47#stat.127A.47.7">127A.47, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.08.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Special education revenue.</span></h2>
          <p>Payment of special education revenue for nonresident pupils enrolled in the state-approved alternative program must be made according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/127A.47#stat.127A.47.7">127A.47, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.08.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.37.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/486/#laws.0.78.0">1988 c 486 s 78</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.4.22.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 4 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 104</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.2.37.0">1998 c 398 art 2 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/78/#laws.2.30.0">2009 c 78 art 2 s 30</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/96/#laws.2.31.0">2009 c 96 art 2 s 31</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.09">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.09 DESIGNATING AND APPROVING A CENTER.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner shall establish a process for state designation and approval of area learning centers that meet the provisions of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.05">123A.05</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.08">123A.08</a>. Any process for designating and approving an area learning center must emphasize the importance of having the area learning center serve students who have dropped out of school, are homeless, are eligible to receive free or reduced- priced meals, have been suspended or expelled, have been declared truant or are pregnant or parents.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.09.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.7.52.0">1988 c 718 art 7 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.25.0">1991 c 130 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.4.27.0">1994 c 647 art 4 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.102.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 102</a>,104; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/55/#laws.9.19.0">2023 c 55 art 9 s 19</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICE AGREEMENTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.12">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.12 AGREEMENT REGARDING ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.12.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Two or more school districts may enter into an agreement pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a> to increase efficiency in the delivery of administrative services and to reduce costs by the provision of an administrative service by a district or an entity created by or specified in the agreement and the purchase of that service by one or more other districts that are a party to that agreement.</p>
          <p>(b) Administrative services include, but are not limited to:</p>
          <p>(1) a superintendent, deputy superintendent, or assistant superintendent;</p>
          <p>(2) a principal, assistant principal, or vice principal; or</p>
          <p>(3) a director, manager, coordinator, supervisor, dean, or other administrative position regardless of title.</p>
          <p>(c) A person providing administrative services to another district pursuant to the agreement shall remain an employee of the employing district or the entity created by or specified in the agreement for all purposes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.12.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Levy.</span></h2>
          <p>If an administrative position is discontinued in a district as a result of the purchase of administrative services under the agreement in subdivision 1, the district may levy over a period of up to three years for costs approved by the commissioner for retirement incentives or severance pay or other costs related to the discontinuance of that position.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.12.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/5/#laws.2.49.0">1Sp2005 c 5 art 2 s 49</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">EDUCATION DISTRICTS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.15">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.15 ESTABLISHING EDUCATION DISTRICTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.15.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The purpose of an education district is to increase educational opportunities for learners by increasing cooperation and coordination among school districts, other governmental units, and postsecondary institutions, and to replace other existing cooperative structures.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.15.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agreement to establish an education district; special provision.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Boards meeting the requirements of subdivision 3 may enter into a written agreement to establish an education district. Once established, cities, counties, and other governmental units as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>, may become members of the education district. The agreement and subsequent amendments must be adopted by majority vote of the full membership of each board.</p>
          <p>(b) The education district agreement may contain a special provision adopted by the vote of a majority of the full membership of each of the boards of the member districts to allow a postsecondary institution or cities, counties, and other governmental units to become a member of the education district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.15.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Requirements for formation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An education district must have one of the following at the time of formation:</p>
          <p>(1) at least five districts;</p>
          <p>(2) at least four districts with a total of at least 5,000 pupils in average daily membership; or</p>
          <p>(3) at least four districts with a total of at least 2,000 square miles.</p>
          <p>Members of an education district must be contiguous. Districts with a cooperation agreement according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.32">123A.32</a> may belong to an education district only as a unit.</p>
          <p>(b) A noncontiguous district may be a member of an education district if the commissioner of education determines that:</p>
          <p>(1) a district between the education district and the noncontiguous district has considered and is unwilling to become a member; or</p>
          <p>(2) a noncontiguous configuration of member districts has sufficient technological or other resources to offer effective levels of programs and services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.15.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Meeting with teacher representatives.</span></h2>
          <p>Before entering into an agreement, the board of each member district must meet and confer with the exclusive representatives of the teachers of each district proposing to enter the education district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.15.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Notice and public hearing on proposed agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>Before entering into an agreement, the board of each member district must publish a summary of the proposed agreement and its effect upon the district at least once in a newspaper of general circulation in the district. The board must conduct a public hearing on the proposed agreement not more than ten days after the notice and at least 30 days before entering into an agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.15.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Service cooperatives to assist in establishing education districts.</span></h2>
          <p>If requested, service cooperatives must provide assistance to districts in establishing education districts. The assistance may include determination of appropriate boundaries of the education district and development of the agreement. The service cooperatives may provide any other services requested by the education district.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.15.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.2.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.6.3.0">1988 c 718 art 6 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.19.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 19</a>-23; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.6.8.0">1990 c 562 art 6 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.6.19.0">1991 c 265 art 6 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.42.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 42</a>; art 13 s 2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 13</a>-15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.138.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.77.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 77</a>-81,104; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.16">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.16 EDUCATION DISTRICT BOARD.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.16.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">School district representation.</span></h2>
          <p>The education district board shall be composed of at least one representative appointed by the school board or governing board of each member. Each representative must be a member of the appointing school or governing board. Each representative shall serve at the pleasure of the appointing board and may be recalled by a majority vote of the appointing board. Each representative shall serve for the term that is specified in the agreement. The board shall select its officers from among its members and shall determine the terms of the officers. The board shall adopt bylaws for the conduct of its business. The board may conduct public meetings via interactive television if the board complies with chapter 13D in each location where board members are present.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.16.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Postsecondary representation.</span></h2>
          <p>The education district board may appoint, with the approval of the member postsecondary institution, a representative from one or more member postsecondary institutions as a member of the education district board. Each postsecondary representative shall serve at the pleasure of the education district board and may be recalled by a majority vote of the education district board. The education district agreement may specify issues on which a postsecondary representative may vote.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.16.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.3.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.24.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/44/#laws.0.1.0">1991 c 44 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.42.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.16.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 104</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.17">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.17 POWERS AND DUTIES OF AN EDUCATION DISTRICT BOARD.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.17.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Coordination.</span></h2>
          <p>An education district board shall coordinate the programs and services of the education district according to the terms of the written agreement. The board shall implement the agreement for delivering educational services defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.21">123A.21</a>, subdivisions 7 and 8, needed in the education district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.17.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Personnel.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may employ personnel as necessary to provide and support the programs and services of the education district. Education district staff shall participate in retirement programs. Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.143#stat.123B.143.1">123B.143, subdivision 1</a>, a member district of an education district may contract with the education district to obtain the services of a superintendent. The person to provide the services need not be employed by the education district or a member district at the time the contract is entered into.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.17.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may enter into contracts with districts and other public and private agencies to provide services needed in the education district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.17.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">General law.</span></h2>
          <p>The board shall be governed, unless specifically provided otherwise, by laws applicable to independent school districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.17.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Advisory council.</span></h2>
          <p>An advisory council, consisting of representatives from the program areas covered by the agreement, shall be appointed by the Education District Board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.17.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Report to members.</span></h2>
          <p>The board shall submit at least an annual report to the member districts and an annual report to the commissioner of education about the activities of the education district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.17.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Discontinuing grades.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of a district that is a member of an education district may discontinue any of kindergarten through grade 12 or part of those grades and provide instruction for those grades or parts of grades within the education district.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.17.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.4.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.25.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 25</a>-27; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.42.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.17.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.82.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 82</a>,83,104; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.18">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.18 EDUCATION DISTRICT AGREEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.18.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Adoption; content; review.</span></h2>
          <p>An education district board shall adopt a comprehensive agreement for continuous learning. The agreement must address methods to improve the educational opportunities available in the education district. The education district board shall review the agreement annually and propose necessary amendments to the member districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.18.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Extended year instruction.</span></h2>
          <p>The agreement may provide opportunities for pupils to receive instruction throughout the entire year and for teachers to coordinate educational opportunities and provide instruction throughout the entire year. A teacher who is employed for the extended year may develop, in consultation with pupils and parents, individual educational programs for not more than 125 pupils.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.18.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Attendance in other districts.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The agreement may provide for a pupil who is a resident of a member district to enroll in programs or courses offered by another member district or transfer to another member district. A pupil and parent shall consult with a career teacher, counselor, or principal before transferring to another district. The agreement shall specify procedures for reimbursement among the member districts. The district of residence shall count all resident pupils who enroll in programs or courses or transfer to another district as its pupils for the purpose of state aid and levy limitations. The agreement shall determine whether transportation is available for pupils enrolled in programs or courses or transferring to another district.</p>
          <p>(b) Paragraph (a) does not limit any rights or duties under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.03">124D.03</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.18.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.5.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.28.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 28</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.6.11.0">1990 c 562 art 6 s 11</a>,12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.3.38.0">1991 c 265 art 3 s 38</a>; art 6 s 20,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.42.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.18.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 104</a>; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/1/9/#laws.1.2.0">1Sp2003 c 9 art 1 s 2</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.19">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.19 TEACHING POSITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.19.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Teacher defined.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, "teacher" does not include a superintendent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.19.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Negotiated plan for filling positions.</span></h2>
          <p>The boards in all member districts and exclusive bargaining representatives of the teachers in all member districts may negotiate a plan for filling positions resulting from implementation of the education district agreement. If the plan is negotiated among the member districts and the exclusive bargaining representative of each member district and unanimously agreed upon, in writing, the education district must include the plan in the education district agreement. If a plan is not negotiated, the education district is governed by subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.19.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Filling positions without a negotiated plan.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When an education district board or a member board is filling a position resulting from implementation of the agreement, the board may offer the position to a teacher currently employed by a member district according to the exchange teacher provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.54">122A.54</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) If the position is not filled by a currently employed teacher, the board must offer the position to an available teacher in the order of seniority in fields of licensure on a combined seniority list of all available teachers in the member districts. For the purpose of establishing a combined seniority list, each district must be considered to have started school each year on the same date. An available teacher is a teacher in a member district who:</p>
          <p>(1) was placed on unrequested leave of absence by a member district, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a>, not more than one year before the initial formation of an education district as a result of an intention to enter into an education district agreement;</p>
          <p>(2) was placed on unrequested leave of absence by a member district, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a>, as a result of implementing the education district agreement, after the formation of the education district; or</p>
          <p>(3) is placed on unrequested leave of absence by a member district, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a>, as a result of implementing the education district, in the same year the position is filled.</p>
          <p>(c) If no currently employed teacher or available teacher accepts the position, the board may fill the position with any other teacher.</p>
          <p>(d) Any teacher who has been placed on unrequested leave of absence or who has been terminated has a right to a position only as long as the teacher has a right to reinstatement in a member district under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.19.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Probation and termination.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.5">122A.40, subdivision 5</a>, a teacher who has acquired continuing contract rights in a member district and who transfers employment from a member district to the education district or to another member district does not have to serve a probationary period. A teacher who is terminated or discharged by a member district according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.9">122A.40, subdivision 9</a> or 13, or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.41#stat.122A.41.6">122A.41, subdivision 6</a>, has no right to any position under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.19.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Determining whether unrequested leave or termination results from implementing agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>When a school board that intends to enter into an education district agreement, and at the time a board that has entered into an education district agreement places a teacher on unrequested leave of absence, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a>, the board must make a determination whether the placement or termination is a result of implementing the education district agreement. That determination must be included in the notice of proposed placement or termination, may be reviewed at a hearing upon request of the teacher, and must be included in the notice of final action of the board. If the determination is not disputed by the teacher before June 1 or the final date required for action by the board, the teacher shall be deemed to acquiesce in the board's determination.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.19.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.8.6.0">1987 c 398 art 8 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.31.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 31</a>,32; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.42.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 42</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.84.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 84</a>-87,104; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/0/83/#laws.1.26.0">2020 c 83 art 1 s 26</a>,27</p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">SERVICE COOPERATIVES; COOPERATIVE<br>CENTERS; INFORMATION CENTERS; COOPERATIVE UNITS</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.21">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.21 SERVICE COOPERATIVES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment of service cooperatives.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Ten service cooperatives, hereafter designated as SCs, are established. Geographical boundaries for each SC shall coincide with those identified in governor's executive orders 8, dated September 1, 1971, and 59, dated May 29, 1973, issued pursuant to the Regional Development Act of 1969, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.381">462.381</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/462.397">462.397</a>, with the following exceptions:</p>
          <p>(1) development regions one and two shall be combined to form a single SC;</p>
          <p>(2) development regions six east and six west shall be combined to form a single SC; and</p>
          <p>(3) development regions seven east and seven west shall be combined to form a single SC.</p>
          <p>(b) The SC shall cooperate with the regional development commission for the region with which its boundaries coincide but shall not be responsible to nor governed by that regional development commission.</p>
          <p>(c) Two or more identified SCs may, upon approval by a majority of the members in each affected SC, be combined and administered as a single SC.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Purpose of SC.</span></h2>
          <p>The primary purposes of designation as a SC shall be to perform planning on a regional basis and to assist in meeting specific needs of clients in participating governmental units which could be better provided by a SC than by the members themselves. The SC must provide those programs and services which are determined, pursuant to subdivision 7, to be priority needs of the particular region and must assist in meeting special needs which arise from fundamental constraints upon individual members.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Membership and participation.</span></h2>
          <p>Full membership in a SC shall be limited to public school districts, cities, counties, and other governmental units as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>, but nonvoting memberships shall be available to nonpublic school administrative units and other partnership agencies or organizations within the SC. A school district, city, county, or other governmental unit or nonprofit organization may belong to one or more SCs. Participation in programs and services provided by the SC shall be discretionary. No school district, city, county, or other governmental unit shall be compelled to participate in these services under authority of this section. Nonpublic school students and personnel are encouraged to participate in programs and services to the extent allowed by law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Governing board.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The care, management, and control of a SC shall be vested in a board of directors composed of not less than six nor more than 15 members. A majority of the members of the SC board of directors shall be current members of school boards of participating public school districts. Election of the school board members to the SC board of directors shall be by vote of all current school board members of participating public school districts with each school board member having one vote. The remaining board members may be representatives at large appointed by the board members or elected as representatives by other participating agencies, such as cities, counties, or other governmental units.</p>
          <p>(b) The election timeline shall be compatible with those for school board members and shall be addressed within the bylaws of each SC.</p>
          <p>(c) A vacancy on the SC board which results in an unexpired term may be filled by appointment by the SC board of directors until such vacancy can be filled at the next board election.</p>
          <p>(d) At the organizational meeting, the SC board shall choose its officers and conduct any other necessary organizational business. The SC board may, at its discretion, appoint up to three members at large to the SC board as ex officio, nonvoting members of the board and shall encourage the advisory participation of a cross-section of school and agency personnel within the SC to the extent allowed by law.</p>
          <p>(e) The officers of the SC board shall be a chair, vice-chair, clerk, and treasurer, no two of whom when possible shall be from the same agency.</p>
          <p>(f) A member of the SC board shall have the same liability applicable to a member of an independent school board or other elected governmental officials.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Duties and powers of SC board of directors.</span></h2>
          <p>The board of directors shall have authority to maintain and operate a SC. Subject to the availability of necessary resources, the powers and duties of this board shall include the following:</p>
          <p>(a) The board of directors shall submit, by June 1 of each year to each participating member, an annual plan which describes the objectives and procedures to be implemented in assisting in resolution of the needs of the SC.</p>
          <p>(b) The SC board of directors shall provide adequate office, service center, and administrative facilities by lease, purchase, gift, or otherwise.</p>
          <p>(c) The SC board of directors shall employ a central administrative staff and other personnel as necessary to provide and support the agreed-upon programs and services. The board may discharge staff and personnel pursuant to applicable provisions of law. SC staff and personnel may participate in retirement programs and any other programs available to public school staff and personnel.</p>
          <p>(d) The SC board of directors may appoint special advisory committees composed of superintendents, central office personnel, building principals, teachers, parents, lay persons, and representatives from cities, counties, and other governmental units.</p>
          <p>(e) The SC board of directors may employ service area personnel pursuant to licensure and certification standards developed by the appropriate state agency such as the commissioner and the Professional Educator Licensing and Standards Board.</p>
          <p>(f) The SC board of directors may enter into contracts with school boards of local districts including school districts outside the SC area.</p>
          <p>(g) The SC board of directors may enter into contracts with other public and private agencies and institutions to provide administrative staff and other personnel as necessary to furnish and support the agreed-upon programs and services.</p>
          <p>(h) The SC board of directors shall exercise all powers and carry out all duties delegated to it by members under provisions of the SC bylaws. The SC board of directors shall be governed, when not otherwise provided, by applicable laws of the state.</p>
          <p>(i) The SC board of directors shall submit an annual evaluation report of the effectiveness of programs and services to the members by September 1 of each year following the previous June 30 in which the programs and services were provided.</p>
          <p>(j) The SC board is encouraged to establish cooperative, working relationships and partnerships with postsecondary educational institutions, other public agencies, business, and industry.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Appointment of advisory council.</span></h2>
          <p>There may be advisory councils selected to give advice and counsel to the SC board of directors. The councils may be composed of representatives from public and nonpublic schools, cities, counties, and other governmental units.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Educational programs and services.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The board of directors of each SC shall submit annually a plan to the members. The plan shall identify the programs and services which are suggested for implementation by the SC during the following year and shall contain components of long-range planning determined by the SC. These programs and services may include, but are not limited to, the following areas:</p>
          <p>(1) administrative services;</p>
          <p>(2) curriculum development;</p>
          <p>(3) data processing;</p>
          <p>(4) distance learning and other telecommunication services;</p>
          <p>(5) evaluation and research;</p>
          <p>(6) staff development;</p>
          <p>(7) media and technology centers;</p>
          <p>(8) publication and dissemination of materials;</p>
          <p>(9) pupil personnel services;</p>
          <p>(10) planning;</p>
          <p>(11) secondary, postsecondary, community, adult, and adult vocational education;</p>
          <p>(12) teaching and learning services, including services for students with special talents and special needs;</p>
          <p>(13) employee personnel services;</p>
          <p>(14) vocational rehabilitation;</p>
          <p>(15) health, diagnostic, and child development services and centers;</p>
          <p>(16) leadership or direction in early childhood and family education;</p>
          <p>(17) community services;</p>
          <p>(18) shared time programs;</p>
          <p>(19) fiscal services and risk management programs, including health insurance programs providing reinsurance or stop loss coverage;</p>
          <p>(20) technology planning, training, and support services;</p>
          <p>(21) health and safety services;</p>
          <p>(22) student academic challenges; and</p>
          <p>(23) cooperative purchasing services.</p>
          <p>An SC is subject to regulation and oversight by the commissioner of commerce under the insurance laws of this state when operating a health reinsurance program pursuant to clause (19) providing reinsurance or stop loss coverage.</p>
          <p>(b) A group health, dental, or long-term disability coverage program provided by one or more service cooperatives may provide coverage to nursing homes licensed under chapter 144A and to boarding care homes licensed under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.50">144.50</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.56">144.56</a> and certified for participation in the medical assistance program located in this state.</p>
          <p>(c) A group health, dental, or long-term disability coverage program provided by one or more service cooperatives:</p>
          <p>(1) must rebid contracts for insurance and third-party administration at least every four years. The contracts may be regional or statewide in the discretion of the SC; and</p>
          <p>(2) may determine premiums for its health, dental, or long-term disability coverage individually for specific employers or may determine them on a pooled or other basis established by the SC.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Technical assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>Service cooperatives shall, to the extent possible, make technical assistance for long-range planning available to school districts upon request and shall establish a common database for local and regional decision making.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Financial support for the service cooperatives.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Financial support for SC programs and services shall be provided by participating members with private, state, and federal financial support supplementing as available. The SC board of directors may, in each year, for the purpose of paying any administrative, planning, operating, or capital expenses incurred or to be incurred, assess and certify to each participating school district, nonpublic school administrative unit, city, county, and other governmental unit its proportionate share of all expenses. This share shall be based upon the extent of participation by each school district, nonpublic school administrative unit, city, county, or other governmental unit and shall be in the form of a service fee. Each participating school district, nonpublic school administrative unit, city, county, or other governmental unit shall remit its assessment to the SC board as provided in the SC bylaws. The assessments shall be paid within the maximum levy limitations of each participating member. No participating member shall have any additional liability for the debts or obligations of the SC except that assessment which has been certified as its proportionate share and any other liability the member assumes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24">123A.24</a>, subdivisions 1 and 2.</p>
          <p>(b) Any property acquired by the SC board is public property to be used for essential public and governmental purposes which shall be exempt from all taxes and special assessments levied by a city, county, state, or political subdivision thereof. If the SC is dissolved, its property must be distributed to the members at the time of the dissolution.</p>
          <p>(c) A member may elect to withdraw participation in the SC by a majority vote of its full board membership and upon compliance with the applicable withdrawal provisions of the SC organizational agreement. The withdrawal shall be effective on the June 30 following receipt by the board of directors of written notification of the withdrawal by February 1 of the same year. Notwithstanding the withdrawal, the proportionate share of any expenses already certified to the withdrawing member for the SC shall be paid to the SC board.</p>
          <p>(d) The SC is a public corporation and agency and its board of directors may make application for, accept, and expend private, state, and federal funds that are available for programs of the members.</p>
          <p>(e) The SC is a public corporation and agency and as such, no earnings or interests of the SC may inure to the benefit of an individual or private entity.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Annual meeting.</span></h2>
          <p>Each SC shall conduct a meeting at least annually for its members.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Joint Powers Act.</span></h2>
          <p>Nothing in this section shall restrict the authority granted to school district boards of education by section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.21.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Health coverage pool comparison shopping.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Service cooperatives must permit school districts and other political subdivisions participating in a service cooperative health coverage pool to solicit bids and other information from competing sources of health coverage at any time other than within five months prior to the end of a master agreement.</p>
          <p>(b) A service cooperative must not impose a fine or other penalty against an enrolled entity for soliciting a bid or other information during the allowed period. The service cooperative may prohibit the entity from participating in service cooperative coverage for a period of up to one year, if the entity leaves the service cooperative pool and obtains other health coverage.</p>
          <p>(c) A service cooperative must provide each enrolled entity with the entity's monthly claims data. This paragraph applies notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/13.203">13.203</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.21.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.25.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.97.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 97</a>,104; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2006/0/255/#laws.0.72.0">2006 c 255 s 72</a>,73; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2017/1/5/#laws.12.22.0">1Sp2017 c 5 art 12 s 22</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.215">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.215 INNOVATIVE TECHNOLOGY COOPERATIVE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.215.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment and organization.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Two or more independent school districts may enter into an agreement to establish an innovative cooperative center to provide for technology and other educational services upon the vote of a majority of the full membership of each of the boards of the districts entering into the agreement. The agreement may also provide for membership by a Minnesota state college or university under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/136F.01">136F.01</a>. When a resolution approving this action has been adopted by the board of a district, the resolution shall be published once in a newspaper of general circulation in the district.</p>
          <p>(b) The agreement may provide for the center to be organized into up to four regions. A region may consist of only school districts, only higher education institutions, or a combination of both.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.215.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Name.</span></h2>
          <p>A public corporation so created shall be known as ....(insert name).... Cooperative Center No. ..... and shall have an identification number assigned according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.56">123A.56</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.215.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Governing board.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The center must be operated by a center board consisting of 12 members. Membership on the center board must be established according to the bylaws and approved by every member of the cooperative.</p>
          <p>(b) The terms of office of the first members of the center board must be determined by lot as follows: one-third of the members for one year, one-third of the members for two years, and the remainder of the members for three years, all terms to expire on June 30 of the appropriate year. Thereafter the terms shall be for three years commencing on July 1 of each year. If a vacancy occurs on the center board, it must be filled by the district, by the members of the appropriate region, or by the higher education members, within 90 days. A person appointed to the center board shall qualify as a center board member by filing with the chair a written certificate of appointment from the appointing school board.</p>
          <p>(c) The first meeting of a center board must be at a time mutually agreed upon by center board members. At this meeting, the center board must choose its officers and conduct any other necessary organizational business. Thereafter, the center board must meet on July 1 of each year or as soon thereafter as practicable pursuant to notice sent to all center board members by the chief executive officer of the center.</p>
          <p>(d) The officers of the center board shall be a chair, vice-chair, clerk, and treasurer, no two of whom when possible shall be from the same school district. The chair shall preside at all meetings of the center board, except that in the chair's absence the vice-chair shall preside. The clerk shall keep a complete record of the minutes of each meeting and the treasurer shall be the custodian of the funds of the center. Insofar as applicable, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.09">123B.09</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.14">123B.14</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.143">123B.143</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.147">123B.147</a>, shall apply to the board and officers of the center.</p>
          <p>(e) A majority of the center board shall be a quorum. Any motion other than adjournment shall pass only upon receiving a majority of the votes of the entire center board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.215.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Center powers and duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The center board shall have the general charge of the business of the center. Where applicable, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.51">123B.51</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.52">123B.52</a>, subdivision 4, shall apply. The center board may not issue bonds on its behalf.</p>
          <p>(b) The center board may furnish technology offerings to any eligible person residing in any participating district and may provide any other educational programs or services agreed upon by the participating members. Academic offerings shall be provided only under the direction of properly licensed academic supervisory personnel.</p>
          <p>(c) The center board must employ an executive director, contract with necessary qualified teachers and administrators, and may discharge the same for cause pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a>. The authority for selection and employment of a director shall be vested in the center board. Notwithstanding the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a> or 11, no individual shall have a right to employment as a director based on seniority or order of employment by the center. The center board may employ and discharge other necessary employees and may contract for other services deemed necessary.</p>
          <p>(d) The center board may prescribe rates of tuition for services provided to nonmember students.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.215.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Finances.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The center board established under this section is a public corporation and agency and may receive and disburse federal, state, and local funds made available to it. A participating school district or member must not have any additional individual liability for the debts or obligations of the center except that assessment which has been certified as its proportionate share in accordance with paragraph (b) and subdivision 4. A member of the center board shall have the liability that is applicable to a member of an independent school district board. Any property, real or personal, acquired or owned by the center board for its purposes shall be exempt from taxation by the state or any of its political subdivisions.</p>
          <p>(b) The center board may, in each year, for the purpose of paying any administrative, planning, operating, or capital expenses incurred or to be incurred, assess and certify to each participating school district its proportionate share of any and all expenses. This share must be based upon an equitable distribution formula agreed upon by the participating districts. Each participating district shall remit its assessment to the center board within 30 days after receipt.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.215.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Laws governing independent school districts apply.</span></h2>
          <p>As of the effective date of the creation of any center as contained in the agreement establishing the center, the organization, operation, maintenance, and conduct of the affairs of the center shall be governed by the general laws relating to independent school districts of the state unless provided otherwise in statute. The center does not have the authority to issue bonds or impose a property tax levy.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.215.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Addition and withdrawal of districts.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon approval by majority vote of a school board and of the center board, an adjoining district may become a member in the center and be governed by the provisions of this section and the agreement in effect. Any participating district may withdraw from the center and from the agreement in effect by a majority vote of the full board membership of the participating district desiring withdrawal and upon compliance with provisions in the agreement establishing the center. Upon receipt of the withdrawal resolution reciting the necessary facts, the center board must file a certified copy with the county auditors of the counties affected. The withdrawal shall become effective at the end of the next following school year, but the withdrawal shall not affect the continued liability of the withdrawing district for liabilities incurred prior to the effective withdrawal date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.215.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>The boards of each participating district may agree to dissolve the center effective at the end of any school year or at an earlier time as they may mutually agree. A dissolution must be accomplished in accordance with any applicable provisions of the agreement establishing the center. Upon receipt of the dissolution resolutions from the boards of the participating districts, the center board shall file a certified copy with the county auditors of the counties affected. The dissolution must not affect the continuing liability of the previously participating districts for any continuing obligations, including unemployment benefits.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.215.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/272/#laws.3.23.0">2014 c 272 art 3 s 23</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.22">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.22 COOPERATIVE CENTERS FOR VOCATIONAL EDUCATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>Two or more independent school districts may enter into an agreement to establish a cooperative center to provide for vocational education and other educational services upon the vote of a majority of the full membership of each of the boards of the districts entering into the agreement. The agreement may also provide for membership by cities, counties, and other governmental units as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>. When a resolution approving this action has been adopted by the board of a district, the resolution shall be published once in a newspaper of general circulation in the district. If a petition for referendum on the question of the district entering into the agreement, containing signatures of qualified voters of the district equal to five percent of the number of voters at the last school district general election, is filed with the clerk of the board within 60 days after publication of the resolution, the board must not enter into the agreement until the question has been submitted to the voters of the district at a special election. This election must be conducted and canvassed in the same manner as school district general elections. If a majority of the total number of votes cast on the question within the district is in favor of the proposition, the board may enter into an agreement to establish the center for purposes described in this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Name.</span></h2>
          <p>A public corporation so created shall be known as ....(insert name).... Cooperative Center No. ..... and shall have an identification number assigned pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.56">123A.56</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Governing board.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The center must be operated by a center board of not less than five members which shall consist of members from boards of each of the participating districts within the center and member cities, counties, and other governmental units, appointed by their respective boards. Each participating district must have at least one member on the center board. The center board must choose an administrative officer to administer center board policy and directives who shall serve as an ex officio member of the board but shall not have a vote.</p>
          <p>(b) The terms of office of the first members of the center board must be determined by lot as follows: one-third of the members for one year, one-third for two years, and the remainder for three years, all terms to expire on June 30 of the appropriate year; provided that if the number of members is not evenly divisible by three, the membership will be as evenly distributed as possible among one, two and three-year terms with the remaining members serving the three-year term. Thereafter the terms shall be for three years commencing on July 1 of each year. If a vacancy occurs on the center board, it must be filled by the appropriate school board within 90 days. A person appointed to the center board shall qualify as a center board member by filing with the chair a written certificate of appointment from the appointing school board.</p>
          <p>(c) The first meeting of a center board must be at a time mutually agreed upon by center board members. At this meeting, the center board must choose its officers and conduct any other necessary organizational business. Thereafter the center board must meet on July 1 of each year or as soon thereafter as practicable pursuant to notice sent to all center board members by the chief executive officer of the center.</p>
          <p>(d) The officers of the center board shall be a chair, vice-chair, clerk and treasurer, no two of whom when possible shall be from the same school district. The chair shall preside at all meetings of the center board except in the chair's absence the vice-chair shall preside. The clerk shall keep a complete record of the minutes of each meeting and the treasurer shall be the custodian of the funds of the center. Insofar as applicable, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.09">123B.09</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.14">123B.14</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.143">123B.143</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.147">123B.147</a>, shall apply to the board and officers of the center.</p>
          <p>(e) Each participating district must have equal voting power with at least one vote. A majority of the center board shall be a quorum. Any motion other than adjournment shall pass only upon receiving a majority of the votes of the entire center board.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Center board powers and duties.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The center board shall have the general charge of the business of the center and the ownership of facilities. Where applicable, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.51">123B.51</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.52#stat.123B.52.4">123B.52, subdivision 4</a>, shall apply. The center board may not issue bonds in its behalf. Each participating district may issue its bonds for the purpose of acquisition and betterment of center facilities in the amount certified by the center board to such participating district in accordance with chapter 475.</p>
          <p>(b) The center board (1) may furnish vocational offerings to any eligible person residing in any participating district; (2) may provide special education for the disabled and disadvantaged; and (3) may provide any other educational programs or services defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.21">123A.21</a>, subdivisions 7 and 8, agreed upon by the participating members. Academic offerings shall be provided only under the direction of properly licensed academic supervisory personnel.</p>
          <p>(c) In accordance with subdivision 5, paragraph (b), the center board shall certify to each participating district the amount of funds assessed to the district as its proportionate share required for the conduct of the educational programs, payment of indebtedness, and all other proper expenses of the center.</p>
          <p>(d) The center board must employ and contract with necessary qualified teachers and administrators and may discharge the same for cause pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40">122A.40</a>. The authority for selection and employment of a director shall be vested in the center board. Notwithstanding the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a> or 11, no individual shall have a right to employment as a director based on seniority or order of employment by the center. The center board may employ and discharge other necessary employees and may contract for other services deemed necessary.</p>
          <p>(e) The center board may provide an educational program for secondary and adult vocational phases of instruction. The high school phase of its educational program must be offered as a component of the comprehensive curriculum offered by each of the participating school districts. Graduation must be from the student's resident high school district. Insofar as applicable, sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120A.22#stat.120A.22.1a">120A.22, subdivision 1a</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.14">120B.14</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/120B.35">120B.35</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/121A.21">121A.21</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.44">122A.44</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.69">122A.69</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.22">123A.22</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24">123A.24</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.02">123B.02</a>, subdivisions 1 to 15 and 17 to 20, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.49">123B.49</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.51">123B.51</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.52">123B.52</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.88">123B.88</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.02">124D.02</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.09">124D.09</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.51">124D.51</a>, shall apply.</p>
          <p>(f) The center board may prescribe rates of tuition for attendance in its programs by adults and nonmember district secondary students.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Financing.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any center board established pursuant to this section is a public corporation and agency and may receive and disburse federal, state, and local funds made available to it. A participating school district or member must not have any additional individual liability for the debts or obligations of the center except that assessment which has been certified as its proportionate share in accordance with paragraph (b) and subdivision 4, paragraphs (a) and (c). A member of the center board shall have such liability as is applicable to a member of an independent school district board. Any property, real or personal, acquired or owned by the center board for its purposes shall be exempt from taxation by the state or any of its political subdivisions.</p>
          <p>(b) The center board may, in each year, for the purpose of paying any administrative, planning, operating, or capital expenses incurred or to be incurred, assess and certify to each participating school district its proportionate share of any and all expenses.
