This share must be based upon an equitable distribution formula agreed upon by the participating districts. Each participating district shall remit its assessment to the center board within 30 days after receipt. The assessments shall be paid within the maximum levy limitations of each participating district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Commissioner approval.</span></h2>
          <p>Before a center begins operation, the commissioner must approve the agreement establishing the center entered into by participating districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Laws governing independent school districts apply.</span></h2>
          <p>As of the effective date of the creation of any center as contained in the agreement establishing the center, the organization, operation, maintenance, and conduct of the affairs of the center shall be governed by the general laws relating to independent school districts of the state unless provided otherwise in statute.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Addition and withdrawal of districts.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon approval by majority vote of a board and of the center board, an adjoining district may become a member in the center and be governed by the provisions of this section and the agreement in effect.</p>
          <p>Any participating district may withdraw from the center and from the agreement in effect by a majority vote of the full board membership of the participating district desiring withdrawal and upon compliance with provisions in the agreement establishing the center. Upon receipt of the withdrawal resolution reciting the necessary facts, the center board must file a certified copy with the county auditors of the counties affected. The withdrawal shall become effective at the end of the next following school year but the withdrawal shall not affect the continued liability of the withdrawing district for bonded indebtedness it incurred prior to the effective withdrawal date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>The boards of each participating district may agree to dissolve a center effective at the end of any school year or at an earlier time as they may mutually agree. A dissolution must be accomplished in accordance with any applicable provisions of the agreement establishing the center. Upon receipt of the dissolution resolutions from the boards of the participating districts, the center board shall file a certified copy with the county auditors of the counties affected. The dissolution must not affect the continuing liability of the previously participating districts for bonded indebtedness incurred prior to the dissolution, or for other continuing obligations, including unemployment benefits.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Existing centers.</span></h2>
          <p>Centers operating pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a> which have been approved by the State Board of Education prior to August 1, 1974, shall be subject to its provisions except subdivision 1. Any changes in center agreements necessary to comply with this section shall be completed within 12 months after August 1, 1974, and filed with the commissioner by the administrator of each center. Centers operating pursuant to Laws 1967, chapter 822, as amended, Laws 1969, chapter 775, as amended, and Laws 1969, chapter 1060, as amended, shall not be subject to the provisions of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.22.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.</h2>
          <p>[Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2007/0/146/#laws.11.19.0">2007 c 146 art 11 s 19</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.22.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1974/0/252/#laws.0.1.0">1974 c 252 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1977/0/447/#laws.5.1.0">1977 c 447 art 5 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1982/0/548/#laws.5.2.0">1982 c 548 art 5 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.7.19.0">1983 c 314 art 7 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/444/#laws.0.0.0">1986 c 444</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/266/#laws.2.10.0">1987 c 266 art 2 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/718/#laws.6.4.0">1988 c 718 art 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.6.23.0">1991 c 265 art 6 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.6.39.0">1992 c 499 art 6 s 39</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.13.18.0">1993 c 224 art 13 s 18</a>-20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/488/#laws.0.8.0">1994 c 488 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.14.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 14</a>,42; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.21.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 21</a>-24; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.13.12.0">1996 c 412 art 13 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.91.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 91</a>-96,104; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/107/#laws.0.66.0">1999 c 107 s 66</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 254 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/343/#laws.0.4.0">2000 c 343 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/489/#laws.10.21.0">2000 c 489 art 10 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/56/#laws.0.1.0">2005 c 56 s 1</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.23">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.23 REGIONAL MANAGEMENT INFORMATION CENTERS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.23.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation.</span></h2>
          <p>Any group of two or more independent, special or common school districts may create a regional management information center pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.21">123A.21</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a> to provide computer services to school districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.23.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Center for districts with alternative systems.</span></h2>
          <p>Districts that operate alternative systems approved by the commissioner according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125B.07#stat.125B.07.4">125B.07, subdivision 4</a>, may create one regional management information center under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>. The center shall have all of the powers authorized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>.</p>
          <p>The center board may purchase or lease equipment. It may not employ any staff but may enter into a term contract for services. A person providing services according to a contract with the center board is not a state employee.</p>
          <p>The department shall provide the center all services that are provided to regional centers formed under subdivision 1, including transferring software and providing accounting assistance.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.23.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Fees.</span></h2>
          <p>Regional management information centers may charge fees to affiliated districts for the cost of services provided to the district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.23.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Financial management information services.</span></h2>
          <p>Regional management information centers may provide financial management information services to cities, counties, towns, or other governmental units at mutually negotiated prices.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.23.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.7.14.0">1980 c 609 art 7 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1981/0/358/#laws.5.4.0">1981 c 358 art 5 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1984/0/463/#laws.7.5.0">1984 c 463 art 7 s 5</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/258/#laws.0.12.0">1987 c 258 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.7.11.0">1987 c 398 art 7 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.11.6.0">1989 c 329 art 11 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.8.11.0">1990 c 562 art 8 s 11</a>-13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.6.4.0">1991 c 265 art 6 s 4</a>,5; art 9 s 23-26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.6.3.0">1992 c 499 art 6 s 3</a>,4; art 8 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.13.10.0">1993 c 224 art 13 s 10</a>,11; art 14 s 16; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/465/#laws.2.1.0">1994 c 465 art 2 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.2.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 2</a>,42; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.12.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 12</a>; art 13 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.13.8.0">1996 c 412 art 13 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1997 c 397 art 5 s 104</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.24">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.24 WITHDRAWING FROM COOPERATIVE UNIT; APPEALING DENIAL OF MEMBERSHIP.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.24.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Distribution of assets and liabilities.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) If a district withdraws from a cooperative unit defined in subdivision 2, the distribution of assets and assignment of liabilities to the withdrawing district shall be determined according to this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) The withdrawing district remains responsible for its share of debt incurred by the cooperative unit according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.02#stat.123B.02.3">123B.02, subdivision 3</a>. The district and cooperative unit may mutually agree, through a board resolution by each, to terms and conditions of the distribution of assets and the assignment of liabilities.</p>
          <p>(c) If the cooperative unit and the district cannot agree on the terms and conditions, the commissioner shall resolve the dispute by determining the district's proportionate share of assets and liabilities based on the district's enrollment, financial contribution, usage, or other factor or combination of factors determined appropriate by the commissioner. If the dispute requires the commissioner to involve an administrative law judge, any fees due to the Office of Administrative Hearings must be equally split between the district and cooperative unit. The assets must be disbursed to the withdrawing district in a manner that minimizes financial disruption to the cooperative unit.</p>
          <p>(d) Assets related to an insurance pool shall not be disbursed to a member district under paragraph (c).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.24.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Cooperative unit defined.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, a cooperative unit is:</p>
          <p>(1) an education district organized under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.15">123A.15</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.19">123A.19</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) a cooperative vocational center organized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.22">123A.22</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) an intermediate district organized under chapter 136D;</p>
          <p>(4) a service cooperative organized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.21">123A.21</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) a regional management information center organized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.23">123A.23</a> or as a joint powers district according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>; or</p>
          <p>(6) a special education cooperative organized under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.24.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Appealing denial of membership to commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>If a cooperative unit as defined in subdivision 2, denies membership in the unit to a district, the district may appeal to the commissioner. The commissioner may require the cooperative unit to grant the district membership.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.24.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1959/1/71/#laws.4.17.0">Ex1959 c 71 art 4 s 17</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1961/0/225/#laws.0.1.0">1961 c 225 s 1</a>;       <div class="section" id="stat.123A.245">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.245 COOPERATIVE UNITS; ELIGIBILITY FOR GRANTS.</h1>
        <p>A cooperative unit, through its governing board, may apply for all competitive grants administered by agencies of the state and other government or nongovernment sources.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.245.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.9.18.0">1999 c 241 art 9 s 18</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.25">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.25 COOPERATIVE UNIT; INSURANCE POOLS.</h1>
        <p>Any cooperative unit defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, that directly manages a health insurance pool or provides health insurance coverage through an insurance pool as a service to members must create a labor-management committee representative of the groups covered by the pool to advise the governmental unit on management matters of the coverage.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.25.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.10.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 104</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.26">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.26 COOPERATIVE UNITS; PROHIBITED AID AND LEVIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.26.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">General prohibition.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless specifically permitted in the provision authorizing an aid or a levy, cooperative units of government defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.24#stat.123A.24.2">123A.24, subdivision 2</a>, are prohibited from making a property tax levy or qualifying for or receiving any form of state aid except as provided in subdivisions 2 and 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.26.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>A cooperative unit may apply for and receive a grant on behalf of its members.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.26.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.</h2>
          <p>MS 2018 [Repealed, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/1/11/#laws.1.26.0">1Sp2019 c 11 art 1 s 26</a>]</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.26.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.6.22.0">1994 c 647 art 6 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.9.27.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 9 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/1/4/#laws.3.11.0">1Sp1997 c 4 art 3 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 104</a>; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.27">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.27 RESERVED REVENUE FOR DISTRICT COOPERATION.</h1>
        <p>A district that was a member of an intermediate school district organized pursuant to chapter 136D on July 1, 1996, must place a portion of its general education revenue in a reserved account for instructional services from entities formed for cooperative services for special education programs and secondary vocational programs. The amount reserved is equal to the levy made according to Minnesota Statutes 1993 Supplement, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124.2727#stat.124.2727.6">124.2727, subdivision 6</a>, for taxes payable in 1994 divided by the actual pupil units in the intermediate school district for fiscal year 1995 times the number of actual pupil units in the school district in 1995. The district must use 5/11 of the revenue for special education and 6/11 of the revenue for secondary vocational education. The district must demonstrate that the revenue is being used to provide the full range of special education and secondary vocational programs and services available to each child served by the intermediate. The secondary vocational programs and services must meet the requirements established in an articulation agreement developed between the commissioner of education and the Board of Trustees of the Minnesota State Colleges and Universities.</p>
        <p>A district that was a member of an education district organized pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.15">123A.15</a> on July 1, 1999, must place a portion of its general education revenue in a reserve account for instructional services from entities formed for cooperative services. Services may include secondary vocational programs, special education programs, staff development, and gifted and talented instruction. The amount reserved is equal to $50 per pupil unit times the actual number of pupil units in the district.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.27.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.11.3.0">1998 c 397 art 11 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.1.32.0">1998 c 398 art 1 s 32</a>; art 5 s 55; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/254/#laws.0.6.0">2000 c 254 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <h1 class="fgh">COOPERATION AND COMBINATION</h1>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.30">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.30 AGREEMENTS FOR SECONDARY EDUCATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.30.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The provisions of this section shall apply to a district with fewer than 375 pupils enrolled in grades 7 to 12.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.30.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>The board may enter into one or more agreements providing for instruction of its secondary pupils in one or more districts. The agreement must be effective on July 1 and shall be for a specified or indefinite number of years. The agreement must set forth the obligations of transportation, the tuition to be paid to the providing district, and all additional charges and fees to be paid to the providing district. The amount of tuition shall not be subject to the provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.488#stat.123A.488.2">123A.488, subdivision 2</a>. The agreement may provide for negotiation of a plan for the assignment or employment in a providing district as an exchange teacher according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.54">122A.54</a>, or placement on unrequested leave of absence of teachers whose positions are discontinued as a result of the agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.30.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Public hearing.</span></h2>
          <p>Before entering into agreements permitted by subdivision 2, the board must hold a public hearing. The board must publish notice of the hearing in the newspaper with the largest circulation in the district. If the board proposes to enter into agreements with two or more districts, the board may conduct separate or consolidated hearings.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.30.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Review and comment.</span></h2>
          <p>After the hearing required by subdivision 3 and before entering into an agreement, the board must submit the agreement to the commissioner for review and comment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.30.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Aid payments.</span></h2>
          <p>A district entering into an agreement permitted in subdivision 2 must continue to count its resident pupils who are educated in other districts as resident pupils in the calculation of pupil units for the purposes of state aids, levy limitations, and any other purpose. A district may continue to provide transportation and collect transportation aid for its resident pupils. For purposes of aid calculations, the commissioner may adjust the cost per eligible pupil transported to reflect changes in cost resulting from the agreement, if any.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.30.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Severance pay.</span></h2>
          <p>A district must pay severance pay to a teacher who is placed on unrequested leave of absence by the district as a result of the agreement. A teacher is eligible under this subdivision if the teacher:</p>
          <p>(1) is a teacher, but not a superintendent;</p>
          <p>(2) has a continuing contract with the district according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.7">122A.40, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
          <p>The amount of severance pay must be equal to the teacher's salary for the school year during which the teacher was placed on unrequested leave of absence minus the gross amount the teacher was paid during the 12 months following the teacher's termination of salary, by an entity whose teachers by statute or rule must possess a valid Minnesota teaching license, and minus the amount a teacher receives as severance or other similar pay according to a contract with the district or district policy. These entities requiring a valid Minnesota teaching license include, but are not limited to, the district that placed the teacher on unrequested leave of absence, another district in Minnesota, an education district, an intermediate school district, a service cooperative, a board formed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a>, a state residential academy, the Perpich Center for Arts Education, a vocational center, or a special education cooperative. These entities do not include a district in another state, a Minnesota public postsecondary institution, or a state agency. Only amounts earned by the teacher as a substitute teacher or in a position requiring a valid Minnesota teaching license shall be subtracted. A teacher may decline any offer of employment as a teacher without loss of rights to severance pay.</p>
          <p>To determine the amount of severance pay that is due for the first six months following termination of the teacher's salary, the district may require the teacher to provide documented evidence of the teacher's employers and gross earnings during that period. The district must pay the teacher the amount of severance pay it determines to be due from the proceeds of the levy for this purpose. To determine the amount of severance pay that is due for the second six months of the 12 months following the termination of the teacher's salary, the district may require the teacher to provide documented evidence of the teacher's employers and gross earnings during that period. The district must pay the teacher the amount of severance pay it determines to be due from the proceeds of the levy for this purpose.</p>
          <p>A teacher who receives severance pay under this subdivision waives all further reinstatement rights under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a> or 11. If the teacher receives severance pay, the teacher shall not receive credit for any years of service in the district paying severance pay prior to the year in which the teacher becomes eligible to receive severance pay.</p>
          <p>The severance pay is subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/465.72">465.72</a>. The district may levy annually according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.43">126C.43</a>, for the severance pay.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1983/0/314/#laws.8.8.0">1983 c 314 art 8 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.6.6.0">1990 c 562 art 6 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 130 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.6.18.0">1991 c 265 art 6 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.12.29.0">1992 c 499 art 12 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.138.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.9.19.0">1996 c 412 art 9 s 19</a>; art 13 s 10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.65.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 65</a>-69,104; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/241/#laws.10.8.0">1999 c 241 art 10 s 8</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.32">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.32 INTERDISTRICT COOPERATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.32.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">District requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The boards of two or more districts may, after consultation with the department, enter into an agreement providing for:</p>
          <p>(1) discontinuance by all districts except one of at least the 10th, 11th, and 12th grades; and</p>
          <p>(2) instruction of the pupils in the discontinued grades in one of the cooperating districts. Each district must continue to operate a school with at least three grades. Before entering into a final agreement, the boards must provide a copy of this agreement to the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.32.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Aid; transportation.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each district must continue to count its resident pupils who are educated in a cooperating district as resident pupils in the calculation of pupil units for all purposes. The agreement must provide for tuition payments between or among the districts.</p>
          <p>(b) Each district must continue to provide transportation and collect transportation aid for its resident pupils pursuant to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.88">123B.88</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.92">123B.92</a>. A district may provide some or all transportation to its resident pupils by contracting with a cooperating district. For purposes of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.92">123B.92</a>, the commissioner may adjust the base cost per eligible pupil transported to reflect changes in costs resulting from the agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.32.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Negotiated plan for teachers whose positions are discontinued.</span></h2>
          <p>The board and exclusive bargaining representative of the teachers in each district discontinuing grades may negotiate a plan to assign or employ in a cooperating district or to place on unrequested leave of absence all teachers whose positions are discontinued as a result of the agreement. The board and exclusive bargaining representative of the teachers in each district providing instruction to nonresident pupils may negotiate a plan to employ teachers from a cooperating district whose positions are discontinued as a result of the agreement. If plans are negotiated and if the boards determine the plans are compatible, the boards shall include the plans in their agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.32.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Combined teacher seniority list.</span></h2>
          <p>If compatible plans are not negotiated before the March 1 preceding any year of the agreement, the cooperating districts shall be governed by this subdivision. Insofar as possible, teachers who have acquired continuing contract rights and whose positions are discontinued as a result of the agreement shall be employed by a cooperating district or assigned to teach in a cooperating district as exchange teachers pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.54">122A.54</a>. If necessary, teachers whose positions are discontinued as a result of the agreement and who have acquired continuing contract rights shall be placed on unrequested leave of absence in fields in which they are licensed in the inverse order in which they were employed by a cooperating district, according to a combined seniority list of teachers in the cooperating districts. For the purpose of establishing a combined seniority list, each district must be considered to have started school each year on the same date.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.32.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Notice; informational meeting.</span></h2>
          <p>Prior to entering into an agreement, the board shall consult with the community at an informational meeting. The board must publish notice of the meeting in the official newspaper of the district and may send written notice of the meeting to parents of pupils who would be affected.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.32.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Meeting location.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding any law to the contrary, boards that have an agreement may hold a valid joint meeting at any location that would be permissible for one of the boards participating in the meeting. A board that has an agreement may hold a meeting in any district that is a party to the agreement. The board shall comply with chapter 13D and any other law applicable to a meeting of a board.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.32.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1979/0/211/#laws.0.4.0">1979 c 211 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1980/0/609/#laws.6.14.0">1980 c 609 art 6 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1985/1/12/#laws.7.12.0">1Sp1985 c 12 art 7 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/384/#laws.2.27.0">1987 c 384 art 2 s 27</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/398/#laws.7.19.0">1987 c 398 art 7 s 19</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.18.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 18</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.32.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 32</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.70.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 70</a>-75,104; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.33">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.33 EMPLOYEES OF COOPERATIVE DISTRICTS UPON DISSOLUTION OR WITHDRAWAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>For the purposes of this section, the terms defined in this subdivision have the meanings given them.</p>
          <p>(a) "Teacher" means a teacher who is employed by a district or center listed in subdivision 2, except that it does not include a superintendent.</p>
          <p>(b) "Cooperative" means any district or center to which this section applies.</p>
          <p>(c) "Withdrawal" means a district's removal of its students from a program of instruction, counseling, or evaluation provided by a cooperative in order to provide the same educational services by other means.</p>
          <p>(d) "Education support position" means a position not requiring a teaching license in which an employee assists a teacher by providing instructional, counseling, or evaluative support services directly to students.</p>
          <p>(e) "Education support employee" means an employee holding an education support position.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>This section applies to:</p>
          <p>(1) an education district organized according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.15">123A.15</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.19">123A.19</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) a cooperative vocational center organized according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.22">123A.22</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) a joint powers district or board organized according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/471.59">471.59</a> which employs teachers to provide instruction;</p>
          <p>(4) an intermediate district organized according to chapter 136D;</p>
          <p>(5) a service cooperative which employs teachers to provide instruction; and</p>
          <p>(6) districts participating in an agreement for the cooperative provision of special education services to children with disabilities according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.11">125A.11</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Agreements for cooperative special education.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Upon the termination of an agreement according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.11">125A.11</a>, a teacher employed to provide special education services by a district participating in the agreement will be afforded rights to employment by other districts according to subdivisions 4, 5, and 6. Nonlicensed employees of a participating district employed to provide special education services will, upon the agreement's termination, be afforded rights to employment by other participating districts according to subdivision 9.</p>
          <p>(b) Upon a district's withdrawal from the cooperative provision of special education under an agreement according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/125A.11">125A.11</a>, a teacher employed to provide special education services by a participating district will be afforded rights to employment by other districts according to subdivisions 4, 7, and 8. Nonlicensed employees of a participating district employed to provide special education services will be afforded rights to employment by the withdrawing district according to subdivision 10.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Notification of teachers.</span></h2>
          <p>In any year in which a cooperative dissolves or a member withdraws from a cooperative, the governing board of a cooperative must provide all teachers employed by the cooperative written notification by March 10 of:</p>
          <p>(1) the dissolution of the cooperative and the effective date of dissolution; or</p>
          <p>(2) the withdrawal of a member of the cooperative and the effective date of withdrawal.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Rights of a teacher with a continuing contract in a member district upon dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This subdivision applies to a teacher previously employed in a member district who:</p>
          <p>(1) had a continuing contract with that member district;</p>
          <p>(2) has been continuously employed immediately after leaving that member district by one or more cooperatives that provided instruction to pupils enrolled in that member district; and</p>
          <p>(3) is either a probationary teacher or has a continuing contract with the cooperative that is dissolving.</p>
          <p>(b) A teacher may elect to resume the teacher's continuing contract with the member district by which the teacher was previously employed by filing a written notice of the election with the member board on or before March 20. Failure by a teacher to file a written notice by March 20 of the year the teacher receives a notice according to subdivision 4 constitutes a waiver of the teacher's rights under this subdivision.</p>
          <p>The member district must make reasonable realignments of positions to accommodate the seniority rights of a teacher electing to resume continuing contract rights in the member district according to this subdivision.</p>
          <p>Upon returning the teacher shall receive credit for:</p>
          <p>(1) all years of continuous service under contract with the cooperative and the member district for all purposes relating to seniority, compensation, and employment benefits; and</p>
          <p>(2) the teacher's current educational attainment on the member district's salary schedule.</p>
          <p>(c) A teacher who does not elect to return to the member district according to this subdivision may exercise rights under subdivision 6.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Rights of other teachers.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This subdivision applies to a teacher who:</p>
          <p>(1) has a continuing contract with the cooperative; and</p>
          <p>(2) either did not have a continuing contract with any member district or does not return to a member district according to the procedures set forth in subdivision 5, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(b) By May 10 of the school year in which the cooperative provides the notice required by subdivision 4, clause (1), the cooperative must provide to each teacher described in subdivision 5 and this subdivision a written notice of available teaching positions in any member district to which the cooperative was providing services at the time of dissolution. Available teaching positions are all teaching positions that, during the school year following dissolution:</p>
          <p>(1) are positions for which the teacher is licensed; and</p>
          <p>(2) are not assigned to a continuing contract teacher employed by a member district after any reasonable realignments which may be necessary under the applicable provisions of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a> or 11, to accommodate the seniority rights of teachers employed by the member district.</p>
          <p>(c) On or before June 1 of the school year in which the cooperative provides the notice required by subdivision 4, clause (1), any teacher wishing to do so must file with the board a written notice of the teacher's intention to exercise the teacher's rights to an available teaching position. Available teaching positions must be offered to teachers in order of their seniority within the dissolved cooperative.</p>
          <p>(d) Paragraph (e) applies to:</p>
          <p>(1) a district that was a member of a dissolved cooperative; or</p>
          <p>(2) any other district that, except as a result of open enrollment according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.03">124D.03</a>, provides essentially the same instruction provided by the dissolved cooperative to pupils enrolled in a former member district.</p>
          <p>(e) For five years following dissolution of a cooperative, a district to which this paragraph applies may not appoint a new teacher or assign a probationary or provisionally licensed teacher to any position requiring licensure in a field in which the dissolved cooperative provided instruction until the following conditions are met:</p>
          <p>(1) a district to which this paragraph applies has provided each teacher formerly employed by the dissolved cooperative, who holds the requisite license, written notice of the position; and</p>
          <p>(2) no teacher holding the requisite license has filed a written request to be appointed to the position with the board within 30 days of receiving the notice.</p>
          <p>If no teacher files a request according to clause (2), the district may fill the position as it sees fit. During any part of the school year in which dissolution occurs and the first school year following dissolution, a teacher may file a request for an appointment according to this paragraph regardless of prior contractual commitments with other member districts. Available teaching positions must be offered to teachers in order of their seniority on a combined seniority list of the teachers employed by the cooperative and the appointing district.</p>
          <p>(f) A teacher appointed according to this subdivision is not required to serve a probationary period. The teacher shall receive credit on the appointing district's salary schedule for the teacher's years of continuous service under contract with the cooperative and the member district and the teacher's educational attainment at the time of appointment or shall receive a comparable salary, whichever is less. The teacher shall receive credit for accumulations of sick leave and rights to severance benefits as if the teacher had been employed by the member district during the teacher's years of employment by the cooperative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Rights of a teacher with a continuing contract in a member district upon withdrawal of the district.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This subdivision applies to a teacher previously employed by a member district who:</p>
          <p>(1) had a continuing contract with the member district which withdraws from a cooperative;</p>
          <p>(2) has been continuously employed immediately after leaving that member district by one or more cooperatives that provided instruction to pupils enrolled in that member district; and</p>
          <p>(3) is either a probationary teacher or has a continuing contract with the cooperative from which the member district is withdrawing.</p>
          <p>(b) A teacher may elect to resume the teacher's continuing contract with the withdrawing district by which the teacher was previously employed by filing a written notice of the election with the withdrawing school board on or before March 20. Failure by a teacher to file written notice by March 20 of the year the teacher receives a notice according to subdivision 4 constitutes a waiver of a teacher's rights under this subdivision.</p>
          <p>The member district must make reasonable realignments of positions to accommodate the seniority rights of a teacher electing to resume continuing contract rights in the member district according to this subdivision.</p>
          <p>Upon returning, the teacher shall receive credit for:</p>
          <p>(1) all years of continuous service under contract with the cooperative and the member district for all purposes relating to seniority, compensation, and employment benefits; and</p>
          <p>(2) the teacher's current educational attainment on the member district's salary schedule.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Rights of a teacher placed on unrequested leave upon withdrawal.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This subdivision applies to a teacher who is placed on unrequested leave of absence, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122A.40#stat.122A.40.10">122A.40, subdivision 10</a> or 11, in the year in which the cooperative provides the notice required by subdivision 4, clause (2), by a cooperative from which a member district is withdrawing.</p>
          <p>This subdivision applies to a district that, except as a result of open enrollment according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124D.03">124D.03</a>, provides essentially the same instruction provided by the cooperative to pupils enrolled in the withdrawing district.</p>
          <p>(b) A teacher shall be appointed by a district to which this subdivision applies to an available teaching position which:</p>
          <p>(1) is in a field of licensure in which pupils enrolled in the withdrawing district received instruction from the cooperative; and</p>
          <p>(2) is within the teacher's field of licensure.</p>
          <p>For the purpose of this paragraph, an available teaching position means any position that is vacant or would otherwise be occupied by a probationary or provisionally licensed teacher.</p>
          <p>(c) A board may not appoint a new teacher to an available teaching position unless no teacher holding the requisite license on unrequested leave from the cooperative has filed a written request for appointment. The request must be filed with the board of the appointing district within 30 days of receiving written notice from the appointing board that it has an available teaching position. If no teacher holding the requisite license files a request according to this paragraph, the district may fill the position as it sees fit. Available teaching positions must be offered to teachers in order of their seniority on a combined seniority list of the teachers employed by the cooperative and the withdrawing member district.</p>
          <p>(d) A teacher appointed according to this subdivision is not required to serve a probationary period. The teacher shall receive credit on the appointing district's salary schedule for the teacher's years of continuous service under contract with the cooperative and the member district and the teacher's educational attainment at the time of appointment or shall receive a comparable salary, whichever is less. The teacher shall receive credit for accumulations of sick leave and rights to severance benefits as if the teacher had been employed by the member district during the teacher's years of employment by the cooperative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Nonlicensed employees upon dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A nonlicensed employee who is terminated by a cooperative that dissolves shall be appointed by a district that is a member of the dissolved cooperative to a position that is created within 36 months of the dissolution of the cooperative and is created as a result of the dissolution of the cooperative. A position must be offered to a nonlicensed employee, who fulfills the qualifications for that position, in order of the employee's seniority within the dissolved cooperative.</p>
          <p>(b) When an education support employee is terminated by a cooperative that dissolves, a district that is a member of the dissolved cooperative must appoint the employee to an education support position if the position is created within 36 months of the dissolution of the cooperative as a result of the dissolution. An education support position must be offered to an education support employee, who fulfills the qualifications for that position, in order of the employee's seniority within the dissolved cooperative.</p>
          <p>(c) An employee appointed according to this subdivision shall receive credit for the employee's:</p>
          <p>(1) continuous years of service with the cooperative on the appointing district's compensation schedule and seniority list; and</p>
          <p>(2) unused sick leave accumulated while employed by the cooperative.</p>
          <p>(d) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/179A.12">179A.12</a> or Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/5510.0510">5510.0510</a>, subparts 1 to 4, a representation petition seeking the exclusive representation of a unit of education support employees employed by a district formerly a member of a dissolved cooperative may be considered by the commissioner of the bureau of mediation services at any time within 11 months of the dissolution of the cooperative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Nonlicensed employees upon withdrawal.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A nonlicensed employee of a cooperative whose active employment is discontinued or reduced as a result of the withdrawal of a member district from the cooperative shall be appointed by the withdrawing member district to a position that is created within 36 months of the withdrawal and is created as a result of the withdrawal of the member district. A position must be offered to a nonlicensed employee, who fulfills the qualifications for that position, in order of the employee's seniority within the cooperative from which a member district withdraws.</p>
          <p>(b) When an education support employee of a cooperative has active employment discontinued or reduced as a result of the withdrawal of a member district from the cooperative, the withdrawing member district must appoint the employee to an education support position if the position is created within 36 months of the withdrawal as a result of the withdrawal of the member district. An education support position must be offered to an education support employee, who meets the qualifications for that position, in order of the employee's seniority within the cooperative from which a member district withdraws.</p>
          <p>(c) An employee appointed according to this subdivision shall receive credit for the employee's:</p>
          <p>(1) continuous years of service with the cooperative on the appointing district's compensation schedule and seniority list; and</p>
          <p>(2) unused sick leave accumulated while employed by the cooperative.</p>
          <p>(d) Notwithstanding section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/179A.12">179A.12</a> or Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/5510.0510">5510.0510</a>, subparts 1 to 4, a representation petition seeking the exclusive representation of a unit of education support employees employed by a member district which has withdrawn from a cooperative may be considered by the commissioner of the bureau of mediation services at any time within 11 months of the district's withdrawal from the cooperative.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.33.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Cooperatives that merge.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Notwithstanding subdivisions 1 to 10, paragraphs (b) and (c) apply to cooperatives that merge.</p>
          <p>(b) If a cooperative enters into an agreement to merge with another cooperative, the boards of the cooperatives and the exclusive representatives of the teachers in the cooperatives and the teachers in each member district may negotiate a plan to assign or employ in a member district or to place on unrequested leave of absence all teachers whose positions are discontinued as a result of the agreement. If plans are negotiated and if the boards determine the plans are compatible, the boards must include the plans in their agreement.</p>
          <p>(c) If compatible plans are not negotiated under paragraph (b) by the March 1 preceding the effective date of the merger of the cooperatives, subdivisions 2 to 10 apply to teachers and nonlicensed employees whose positions are terminated as a result of an agreement to merge cooperatives.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.33.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.9.33.0">1991 c 265 art 9 s 33</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/363/#laws.2.6.0">1992 c 363 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.6.7.0">1993 c 224 art 6 s 7</a>; art 9 s 20,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.6.3.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 6 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/412/#laws.13.11.0">1996 c 412 art 13 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.76.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 76</a>,104; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.35">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.35 COOPERATION AND COMBINATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.35.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Scope.</span></h2>
          <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.35">123A.35</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.43">123A.43</a> establish procedures for boards that adopt, by resolution, a five-year written agreement:</p>
          <p>(1) to provide at least secondary instruction cooperatively for at least one or two years, if the districts cooperate according to subdivision 2; and</p>
          <p>(2) to combine into one district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.35.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Cooperation requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Cooperating districts must:</p>
          <p>(1) implement a written agreement according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.32">123A.32</a> no later than the first year of cooperation;</p>
          <p>(2) all be members of one education district, if any one of the districts is a member, no later than the end of the second year of cooperation; and</p>
          <p>(3) all be members of one service cooperative, if any one of the districts is a member.</p>
          <p>Clause (1) does not apply to a district that implemented an agreement for secondary education, according to Minnesota Statutes 1996, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/122.535">122.535</a>, during any year before the 1991-1992 school year. If the districts cooperate for one or more years, the agreement may be continued during those years.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.35.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Combination requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Combining districts must be contiguous and meet one of the following requirements at the time of combination:</p>
          <p>(1) at least two districts with at least 400 resident pupils enrolled in grades 7 through 12 in the combined district and projections, approved by the department, of enrollment at least at that level for five years;</p>
          <p>(2) at least two districts if either:</p>
          <p>(i) both of the districts qualify for secondary sparsity revenue under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/126C.10#stat.126C.10.7">126C.10, subdivision 7</a>, and have an average isolation index over 23; or</p>
          <p>(ii) the combined district qualifies for secondary sparsity revenue;</p>
          <p>(3) at least three districts with fewer than 400 resident pupils enrolled in grades 7 through 12 in the combined district; or</p>
          <p>(4) at least two districts with fewer than 400 resident pupils enrolled in grades 7 through 12 in the combined district if either district is located on the border of the state.</p>
          <p>A combination under clause (2), (3), or (4) must be approved by the commissioner. The commissioner shall disapprove a combination under clause (2), (3), or (4) if the combination is educationally unsound or would not reasonably enable the districts to fulfill statutory and rule requirements.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.35.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.5.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/356/#laws.0.48.0">1989 c 356 s 48</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.6.4.0">1990 c 562 art 6 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.6.10.0">1991 c 265 art 6 s 10</a>,11; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.6.8.0">1992 c 499 art 6 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.13.14.0">1993 c 224 art 13 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/305/#laws.1.138.0">1996 c 305 art 1 s 138</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.34.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 34</a>,104; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.36">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.36 COOPERATION AND COMBINATION PLAN.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Adoption and commissioner review.</span></h2>
          <p>Each board must adopt, by resolution, a plan for cooperation and combination. The plan must address each item in this section. The plan must be specific for any item that will occur within three years and may be general or set forth alternative resolutions for an item that will occur in more than three years. The plan must be submitted to the commissioner of education and the secretary of state for review and comment. Significant modifications and specific resolutions of items must be submitted to the commissioner for review and comment. In the official newspaper of each district proposed for combination, the board must publish at least a summary of the adopted plans, each significant modification and resolution of items, and each commissioner review and comment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Rule exemptions.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must identify the rules of the commissioner of education from which the district intends to request exemption, according to Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/3500.1000">3500.1000</a>. The plan may provide information about state laws that deter or impair cooperation or combination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Board formation.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must state:</p>
          <p>(1) whether the new district would have one elected board or whether it would have one elected board and one elected board for each elementary school exercising powers and duties delegated to it by the board of the entire district;</p>
          <p>(2) how many of the existing members of each district would become members of the board of the combined district and, if so, a method to gradually reduce the membership to six or seven; and</p>
          <p>(3) if desired, election districts that include the establishment of separate areas from each of the combining districts from which board members will be elected, the boundaries of these election districts, and the initial term of the member elected from each of these election districts.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Administration.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must provide for:</p>
          <p>(1) selection of one superintendent for the combined district at a specified time, according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.143#stat.123B.143.1">123B.143, subdivision 1</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) alterations, if any, in administrative personnel and duties.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Employees.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must state:</p>
          <p>(1) procedures needed, at the time of combination, to combine teachers into one bargaining unit, with the exclusive representative determined according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.75">123A.75</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) procedures needed, at the time of combination, to combine other bargaining units;</p>
          <p>(3) procedures to negotiate, with the assistance of the Bureau of Mediation Services, an employment plan for licensed employees affected by the agreement;</p>
          <p>(4) procedures to negotiate, with the assistance of the Bureau of Mediation Services, an employment plan for nonlicensed employees affected by the agreement; and</p>
          <p>(5) incentives that may be offered to superintendents, principals, teachers, and other licensed and nonlicensed employees, such as early retirement, severance pay, and health insurance benefits.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Academic programs.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must set forth:</p>
          <p>(1) elementary curriculum and programs;</p>
          <p>(2) improvements in secondary course offerings in at least communications, mathematics, science, social studies, foreign languages, physical education, health, and career education;</p>
          <p>(3) procedures for involving parents, teachers, and other interested people in developing learner outcomes in curricular areas;</p>
          <p>(4) procedures for involving teachers in determining levels of learner outcomes;</p>
          <p>(5) implications for special education cooperatives, secondary vocational cooperatives, joint powers agreements, education districts, and other cooperative arrangements if the districts combined and if they did not; and</p>
          <p>(6) a description of the long-range educational services of the combined district and of the individual districts if the combination is not achieved.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Pupil activities.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must provide for combining extracurricular and cocurricular activities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Referendum.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must set forth:</p>
          <p>(1) procedures for a referendum, held prior to the year of the proposed combination, to approve combining the districts; and</p>
          <p>(2) whether a majority of those voting in each district proposed for combination or a majority of those voting on the question in the entire area proposed for combination would be needed to pass the referendum.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Finances.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must state:</p>
          <p>(1) whether debt service for the bonds outstanding at the time of combination remains solely with the district that issued the bonds or whether all or a portion of the debt service for the bonds will be assumed by the combined district and paid by the combined district on behalf of the district that issued the bonds;</p>
          <p>(2) whether obligations for a capital loan or energy loan made according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216C.37">216C.37</a> or sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.292">298.292</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/298.297">298.297 </a>outstanding at the time of combination remain solely with the district that obtained the loan, or whether all or a portion of all the loan obligations will be assumed by the combined district and paid by the combined district on behalf of the district that obtained the loan;</p>
          <p>(3) the treatment of debt service levies, down payment levies under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.63">123B.63</a>, and referendum levies;</p>
          <p>(4) whether the cooperating or combined district will levy for reorganization operating debt according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123B.82">123B.82</a>, clause (1); and</p>
          <p>(5) two- and five-year projections, prepared by the department upon the request of any district, of revenues, expenditures, and property taxes for each district if it cooperated and combined and if it did not.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Building sites.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must provide for:</p>
          <p>(1) locations for elementary schools which need not be altered and may contain assurances that, to the extent feasible, elementary schools will be retained for at least the number of years specified in the plan; and</p>
          <p>(2) one location, if possible, for a secondary school.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.36.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Timing.</span></h2>
          <p>The plan must contain a time schedule for implementation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.36.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.6.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.6.12.0">1991 c 265 art 6 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.6.9.0">1992 c 499 art 6 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.6.5.0">1993 c 224 art 6 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/8/#laws.0.2.0">1995 c 8 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.35.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 35</a>-38,104; art 11 s 3; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/398/#laws.5.55.0">1998 c 398 art 5 s 55</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/182/#laws.1.23.0">2018 c 182 art 1 s 23</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.37">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.37 COMMISSIONER AND VOTER APPROVAL.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.37.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Commissioner approval.</span></h2>
          <p>Before submitting the question of combining districts to the voters at a referendum, the cooperating districts must submit the proposed combination to the commissioner. The commissioner shall determine the date for submission and may require any information it determines necessary. The commissioner shall disapprove the proposed combination if it is educationally unsound, will not reasonably enable the combined district to fulfill statutory and rule requirements, or if the plan or modifications are incomplete. If disapproved by the commissioner, the referendum shall be postponed, but not canceled, by the boards.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.37.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Voter approval.</span></h2>
          <p>A referendum on the question of combination must be conducted during the first or second year of cooperation for districts that cooperate according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.35">123A.35</a>, or no more than 18 months before the effective date of combination for districts that do not cooperate. The referendum must be on a date called by the boards. The referendum must be conducted by the boards according to the Minnesota Election Law, as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/200.01">200.01</a>. If the referendum fails, the same question or a modified question may be submitted. If the referendum fails again, the districts must modify their cooperation and combination plan. A third referendum may be conducted. If a second or third referendum is conducted after October 1, the newly combined district may not levy under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.39#stat.123A.39.3">123A.39, subdivision 3</a>, until the following year. Referendums shall be conducted on the same date in all districts.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.37.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.7.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.6.5.0">1990 c 562 art 6 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.6.13.0">1991 c 265 art 6 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.6.10.0">1992 c 499 art 6 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.6.6.0">1993 c 224 art 6 s 6</a>; art 13 s 15; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.39.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 39</a>,104; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.38">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.38 EFFECTIVE DATE OF COMBINATION.</h1>
        <p>The effective date for combination of districts shall be July 1.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.38.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.8.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 8</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.104.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 104</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.39">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.39 EMPLOYEES OF COOPERATING AND COMBINING DISTRICTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.39.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Combined seniority list.</span></h2>
          <p>During the school year before the cooperation begins and during the school years of cooperation, the districts shall comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.32#stat.123A.32.4">123A.32, subdivision 4</a>, unless compatible plans are negotiated according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.32#stat.123A.32.3">123A.32, subdivision 3</a>. The districts shall comply with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.75">123A.75</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.39.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Nonlicensed employees termination.</span></h2>
          <p>If compatible plans are not negotiated according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.36#stat.123A.36.5">123A.36, subdivision 5</a>, the boards shall comply with this subdivision with respect to nonlicensed employees. Nonlicensed employees whose positions are discontinued as a result of cooperation or combination, as applicable, shall be:</p>
          <p>(1) employed by a cooperating board or the combined board, if possible;</p>
          <p>(2) assigned to work in a cooperating district or the combined district, if possible; or</p>
          <p>(3) terminated in the inverse order in which they were employed in a district, according to a combined seniority list of nonlicensed employees in the cooperating or combined district, as applicable.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.39.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Retirement and severance levy.</span></h2>
          <p>A cooperating or combined district that levied under Minnesota Statutes 1996, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/124.2725#stat.124.2725.3">124.2725, subdivision 3</a>, for taxes payable in 1995 may levy for severance pay or early retirement incentives for licensed and nonlicensed employees who retire early as a result of the cooperation or combination.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.39.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Employment laws.</span></h2>
          <p>Unless otherwise explicitly provided, chapter 179A governs the rights and duties of employers and employees. Either party may promptly submit questions of procedure, interpretation, or application to the commissioner of mediation services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.39.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.9.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 9</a>,37; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1990/0/562/#laws.6.22.0">1990 c 562 art 6 s 22</a>-26; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/130/#laws.0.37.0">1991 c 130 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/265/#laws.6.34.0">1991 c 265 art 6 s 34</a>-38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/499/#laws.6.16.0">1992 c 499 art 6 s 16</a>,17; art 12 s 29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/0/224/#laws.6.9.0">1993 c 224 art 6 s 9</a>-15; art 13 s 37,38; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1994/0/647/#laws.1.11.0">1994 c 647 art 1 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.6.6.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 6 s 6</a>-9; art 16 s 13; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.1.53.0">1997 c 7 art 1 s 53</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.40.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 40</a>,98,104; art 11 s 3</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.40">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.40 COUNTY AUDITOR PLAT.</h1>
        <p>Upon the request of two or more districts that have adopted a resolution to cooperate and combine, the county auditor shall prepare a plat. If the proposed combined district is located in more than one county, the request must be submitted to the county auditor of the county that has the greatest land area in the proposed district. The plat must show:</p>
        <p>(1) the boundaries of each of the present districts;</p>
        <p>(2) the boundaries of the proposed district;</p>
        <p>(3) the boundaries of proposed election districts, if requested; and</p>
        <p>(4) other information deemed pertinent by the boards or the county auditor.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.123A.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/329/#laws.6.10.0">1989 c 329 art 6 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/397/#laws.5.41.0">1998 c 397 art 5 s 41</a>,104</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.123A.41">
        <h1 class="shn">123A.41 LEVIES FOR DISTRICTS AT TIME OF COMBINATION.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.41.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Referendum revenues.</span></h2>
          <p>The referendum revenue authorization of the combined district shall be one of the methods set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.73#stat.123A.73.4">123A.73, subdivision 4</a>, 5, or 6, and must be consistent with the plan adopted according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.36">123A.36</a>, and any subsequent modifications.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.41.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Bonded debt.</span></h2>
          <p>Debt service for bonds outstanding at the time of the combination may be levied by the combined board consistent with the plan adopted according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.36">123A.36</a>, and any subsequent modifications, subject to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/475.61">475.61</a>. The primary obligation to pay the bonded indebtedness that is outstanding on the effective date of combination remains with the district that issued the bonds. However, the combined district may make debt service payments on behalf of a preexisting district.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.123A.41.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Capital loan.</span></h2>
          <p>The combined board may levy for the obligations for a capital loan outstanding at the time of combination, consistent with the plan adopted according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/123A.36">123A.36</a> and any subsequent modifications.
